| Money word problems to 100 — consolidation: number cards 30₮-90₮; word problems — mother twisted 20 ropes, sister twisted 10 more than mother, how many did sister twist? Bolor's 40 books are 10 fewer than her notebooks, how many notebooks? A competition has 20 girls and the same number of boys, how many children?; add/subtract grids | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 114 |
| Three-digit money problems — Khulan has 975 tögrög and Margad has 895 tögrög; the chosen goods are shown as pictures with prices (ice creams 500₮ and 300₮, a cup sweet 535₮, lollipops 200₮, 300₮, 400₮, chewing gum packs 150₮, 60₮, 240₮, a book 90₮); show Khulan's and Margad's chosen things in a table — can they buy their chosen things from the food shop? (Khulan's total worked: 300 + 400 + 150 = 3 hundreds + 4 hundreds + 1 hundred 5 tens = 8 hundreds 5 tens = 800 + 50 = 850; Margad's: 500 + 200 + 90 = 790); b. if Margad chose the cup sweet and the chocolate egg — calculate how much she would pay (535 + 140 worked in the place table and column: add the ones 5 + 0, the tens 3 + 4, the hundreds 5 + 1, giving 675); c. if Khulan chose the 3 cheapest products, how many tögrög will she have left? (ice cream 300₮, sweet 200₮, gum 60₮: 300 + 200 + 60 = 560₮; find the remaining money: 975 − 560 worked in column steps at the top of printed p. 104 — the step results are right (ones 5, tens 1, hundreds 4, giving 415) but the two step equations print plus signs where minus signs belong ("5н + 0н = 5н" and "7а + 6а = 1а" under captions that say "subtract") — see suspected-error note); d. if Margad buys those pictured things, how many tögrög will she have left? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 103 |
| Money problem solving — Saruul has 350 tögrög — what can she buy from the shop (a sweet 200₮, coffee 300₮, gum 50₮, a lollipop 100₮ pictured)?; count how many tögrög there are and compare (stacks of 100₮, 50₮, 20₮, 10₮ notes joined by "and"); find the expressions' values (231 − 256 + 48 as printed — see suspected-error note; 92 + 12 × 6; 198 − 15 × 4; 52 × 2 − 100; 106 ÷ 2 + 81; 632 − 562 + 158); Temuulen will buy his friend a birthday present: he has 500 tögrög (one 100₮ note and four 100₮ notes pictured) — which of the pictured things can he buy (a book 650₮, a toy car 500₮, a gift box 350₮, a basket of sweets 450₮)? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 106 |
| Revision: column addition with prices — Bat and Tuya each have 1000 tögrög and went to a stationery shop together (a price table: oil pens 50, 100, 200₮; ball pens 300, 450, 800₮; pen refills 150, 200, 250₮; pencils 120, 320, 550₮; glue 230, 400₮; notebooks 120, 240, 300₮; rulers 350, 420, 550₮; brushes 420, 580, 600₮; erasers 150, 360, 750₮; pencil cases 350, 549₮): there are notebooks of many prices — 120, 240, 300 tögrög; a. when buying a 549₮ pencil case and a 50₮ oil pen, how many tögrög are paid in all? b. when buying the cheapest ruler and a 549₮ pencil case, how much in all? c. when buying a 580₮ brush and a 350₮ ruler, how much in all?; worked example 1: find the total paid for a 549₮ pencil case and a 50₮ oil pen and explain the calculation: 549 + 50 = 500 + 40 + 9 + 50 = 500 + 90 + 9 = 599, shown in the place table and in column steps (first add the ones: 9 ones + 0 ones = 9 ones; then the tens: 4 tens + 5 tens = 9 tens; then the hundreds: 5 hundreds + 0 hundreds = 5 hundreds); worked example 2: calculate the total for the cheapest ruler and a 549₮ pencil case: the column addition 549 + 350 is built in three stages — ones 9, tens 9 (4 + 5), hundreds 8 (5 + 3) — giving 899 | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 6 |
| Adding whole thousands of tögrög — goods are pictured with prices (a book of Mongolian traditional games 6000₮; a notebook 3000₮; plasticine 2000₮): a. when buying the story book (үлгэрийн ном) and the plasticine, how many tögrög are paid in all? (shown with 1000₮ notes and a bar model: the story book's price 6000 tögrög, the plasticine's price 2000 tögrög: write the expression; how is the sum's value found? 6 thousand + 2 thousand = … thousand; 6000 + 2000 = …); b. what is the price difference between the story book and the notebook? (bar model: 6000 and 3000; to find the price difference, find the difference's value; 6 thousand − 3 thousand = … thousand; 6000 − 3000 = …); calculate (4 + 3; 40 + 30; 400 + 300; 4000 + 3000; 300 + 500; 3000 + 500; 1000 + 7000; 9 − 5; 90 − 50; 900 − 500; 9000 − 5000; 500 − 100; 5000 − 1000; 8000 − 2000; 10000 − 3000); for the story book, the plasticine and the notebook: which one's price differs from the total price of the other two, and by how much? Explain how you found it | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 24 |
| Price problems: finding missing data — do the shopping calculation: first research and find how many tögrög the price of each one is (a table: flour — measurement unit kg, quantity 1; rice — kg, 1; bread — pieces, 2; the unit price and total price columns are empty to be filled from real prices; a total price row); for each of the following problems, say what information is lacking: complete the information and answer the questions: a. 3 kg of potatoes were bought: how many tögrög were paid to the seller? (the price of 1 kg is missing); b. grandmother bought … loaves of bread: if 1 loaf is 900 tögrög, how many tögrög did grandmother pay in all? (the number of loaves is missing); solve the problems and explain how you solved them (a table: a juice drink — unit price 300₮, quantity 2, total ?; chewing gum — unit price ?, quantity 5, total 250₮; chocolate — 500₮, quantity ?, total 500₮; an eraser — 200₮, quantity ?, total 400₮; a pencil — ?, quantity 2, total 800₮; writing paper — ?, quantity 8, total 400₮; plain paper — ?, quantity 4, total 200₮); mother bought 1 kg of apples and 1 kg of pears: 1 kg of pears is 5640₮ and apples are 2 times cheaper than pears: how many tögrög did mother pay in all (5640 + 2820 = 8460)?; a "Goi" drink costs 700₮, "Pure water" 350₮ and "Apple juice" 650₮: a pupil on a trip bought 4 Goi drinks, 6 apple juices and 8 pure waters: how many tögrög did he pay in all (9500)?; a juice drink costs 250₮; bagged nuts are 4 times more expensive than the juice, and bagged raisins are 5 times cheaper than the nuts: find the price of each one (250₮, 1000₮, 200₮); sea-buckthorn drink at 150₮ and a juice drink at 250₮ are given for each pupil's lunch: if there are 10 pupils in the class, how many tögrög are spent on one day's lunch? What if there are 20 pupils (4000₮; 8000₮)?; 5 boxes of apples, each weighing 12 kg, and 3 boxes of raisins were put into a store room: if the total weight of the apples and raisins put in was 90 kg, what is the weight of one box of raisins ((90 − 60) ÷ 3 = 10 kg)?; a shop has 960 notebooks, and the pens are 4 times more than the notebooks: how many notebooks and pens are there in all? The pens are how many times more than the notebooks (4800 in all)? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 103 |
| Multiplying and dividing by tens (Аравтаар үржүүлэх, хуваах): the children made savings by handing in waste paper: calculate how many tögrög the children's collected hundred-tögrög and thousand-tögrög notes make in total (the table: 23 notes of 100₮: 23 × 100 = ?; 120 notes of 1000₮: 120 × 1000 = ?, with place grids showing the digits shifting: 23 becomes 2300 and 120 becomes 120000; the answer: in all □ tögrög); when a number is multiplied by 10 (100, 1000), how does the digits' value change (the column multiplication 234 × 10 is worked by places: 4 ones × 10 = 40, 3 tens × 10 = 300, 2 hundreds × 10 = 2000, giving 2340; and the shift diagram: 234 → 2340 by ×10, → 23400 by ×100, → 234000 by ×1000; a boy's rule: "When a number is multiplied by 10, the digits' value moves forward 1 place; by 100, it moves 2 places; by 1000, it moves 3 places"); calculate the money in the wallet (127, 132 and 213 notes of the pictured denominations); discuss how you multiplied with your friend and write the conclusion (the column patterns: 34 × 2 = 68; 340 × 20 = 6800; 1860 × 400 = 744000 — multiplying numbers ending in zeros); express the measure in the indicated unit (95 m = □ cm; 43 cm = □ mm; 127 km = □ m; 36 dm = □ cm; the reference: 1 cm = 10 mm, 1 m = 100 cm); find the products' values (the chains: 27 mm ×10 → □ cm ×10 → □ dm, also written ×100; 14 mm ×100 → □ cm ×100 → □ m); following the example of breaking up 5000₮, break up 10000₮ (the diagrams: in the place grid, 5000 ÷ 100 = 50 and 5000 ÷ 1000 = 5; the pictured 5000₮ note exchanges for 5 thousand-tögrög notes or 50 hundred-tögrög notes; the 10000₮ note is to be exchanged the same way); compare the action of dividing by 10 and 100 with the action of multiplying by 10 and 100 and discuss: draw a conclusion (the diagram of 234 with its zeros: dividing by 10 moves the digits' value back 1 place, by 100 back 2 places, by 1000 back 3 places); discuss how a number ending in zeros is divided by tens (240 ÷ 80 = 24 ÷ 8 = 3; 64000 ÷ 800 = 640 ÷ 8 = 80; 15000 ÷ 3000 = 5; a girl's rule: "When a number ending in zeros is divided by tens (hundreds), the quotient is found by dividing the dividend and the divisor each by tens (hundreds)" — cancelling the zeros); find the quotients' values (810 ÷ 90; 81000 ÷ 900; 24000 ÷ 300); express the measure in the indicated unit (4200 cm = □ m; 700 mm = □ cm; 25 m = □ cm; □ mm = 21 cm; the reference box: 10 cm = 1 dm; 1000 m = 1 km); using the skill of multiplying a number by 10, multiply 140, 458 and 760 by 5 and by 9 (a girl's methods: 140 × 5 = 140 × 10 ÷ 2 = 1400 ÷ 2 = 700; 140 × 9 = 140 × 10 − 140 = 1400 − 140 = 1260); find the expressions' values (with ● = 10 and ▲ = 100: (320 × ● + 280 × ●) ÷ ▲; with ■ = 400 and ▶ = 20: 5600 ÷ ■ × ▶ − 1400); an activity: carry out the following actions on the number machine and discuss by how much the screen's result is divisible (a. entering 50 × 10 = = = …; b. entering 2 × 10 = = …) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 30–31 |
| Solving problems when shopping (Худалдаа хийхдээ бодлого бодох): how much of the following goods can a pupil buy with 10000₮ (the pictured goods with their prices: a ruler 500₮, a pen 1200₮, a pencil 200₮, an eraser 700₮, glue 400₮: a. from the most expensive good, at most how many can be bought?; b. from the cheapest good, at most how many?; c. from each good, starting from the most expensive, at most how many pieces?; d. from each good, the most equal number of pieces?); complete the table (a. while the total price to pay stays fixed (unchanged) at 100000₮, when the book's unit price rises, how does the number of books change: the unit prices 1000₮, 5000₮, 10000₮, 20000₮ against the numbers 100, 20, 10, 5; discuss in pairs and write the fitting conclusion about rising and falling (the box: "When a good's unit price rises, the number of goods bought with the money …… . And when the unit price falls, the number of goods bought with the money ……"); b. while the number of goods stays fixed, when the book's unit price rises, how does the total price change: explain the dependence, and how the total price to pay changes when the unit price falls with the number constant); rewrite the torn calculation page neatly and help your friend (the goods table: boards (Самбар) 12 pieces at 155000₮; chairs (Сандал) 17 at 12700₮; tables (Ширээ) 13 at 25259₮ — the totals column is torn away and must be recomputed); a pen is 5 times more expensive than a pencil, and the pen and pencil together cost 1200₮: what does each cost (the pencil 200₮ and the pen 1000₮); if the pen's price rises by 100₮, by how much does the total price to pay for the pen and the pencil change; write each one's price yourselves and complete the table (the food goods: ice cream (Зайрмаг) 2, bread (Талх) 3, a drink (Ундаа) 5, a cake (Бялуу) 4; the rows: the number, the unit price and the total price, with the prices invented by the pupils); discuss in pairs and complete the table (the goods table with the measure unit, amount, unit price and total price to pay: rice by the kilogram, 12 at 2155₮; eggs by the piece, 30 at 550₮; milk by the litre, 15 at 1500₮); a teahouse bought flour at 1400₮ per kilogram at the food market: explain how the total price to pay changes as the amount grows (the scaling diagrams: 25 kg multiplied by 4 to 100 kg with their totals, and 1000 kg divided by 5 with its total); observe the dependence tables and complete them (a. 10 kg against 8000₮, with the amount doubled against 16000₮; b. 15 m against 15000₮, with 5 m against the unknown price); the school canteen bought the following food goods for one day's need: put the fitting numbers in the table (beef (Үхрийн мах) 35 kg with the total 75250₮ and the unit price to find; mutton (Хонины мах) 28 kg at 1950₮ with the total to find; flour (Гурил) 45 with the total 21600₮; rice (Будаа) 25 at 560₮; potatoes (Төмс) at 500₮ with the total 33500₮; and the grand total row); a self-check: 1. compose a problem fitting the expression and solve it (a. 30000₮ ÷ 50 kg = □₮; b. 30000₮ ÷ 5000₮ = □ pieces); 2. friends paid 4000₮ for 5 ice creams: if 10 and 15 ice creams are bought, how many tögrög must be paid for each (800₮ an ice cream, so 8000₮ and 12000₮) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 46–47 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 65 countries (Bangladesh, China, Pakistan, Libya, Kuwait, Laos, North Macedonia, Vietnam, Yemen, Ghana, Sierra Leone, South Africa, Iran, Egypt, Cambodia, Kazakhstan, Nicaragua, Jordan, Ethiopia, Nepal, South Sudan, Mauritania, Greece, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Palestine, Turkey, Tanzania, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Azerbaijan, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria, Eritrea, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Guyana, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Iceland, Peru, Serbia, Suriname, Papua New Guinea, Sweden, Timor-Leste, Uganda).** Bangladesh teaches coins of 1, 2 and 5 taka and notes to 100 taka in Class 1, exchanging 200-, 500- and 1000-taka notes in Class 2, and 100 paisa = 1 taka with taka–paisa sums in Class 3. China teaches the RMB in Grade 1 — yuan, jiao and fen in tens, exchanging notes and reading 59.90 yuan as 59 yuan 9 jiao. Pakistan recognises Rs coins and notes in its Primer, then in Grade 1 makes Rs 50 and Rs 100, asks "is the money enough?", and adds and subtracts prices to Rs 100. Libya recognises coins and notes in Grade 1 and builds on 1 dinar = 1,000 dirhams in Grades 2 and 3, with money problems in all four operations by Grade 7. Kuwait teaches money, coins and paper money across Grades 1–5 Term 2, with its Grade 3 p.147 item taught corrected — it has no correct option as printed. Laos starts money in Grade 2 with banknotes, paying and change in whole kip, and lists notes from 100 to 50,000 kip in Grade 3. North Macedonia teaches denar coins and the 10-denar note in Grade 1 and spends them at a school bazaar. Vietnam introduces its money in Grade 2 and returns to it in Grade 3, as introductions as filed. Yemen recognises riyal coins and notes in Grade 1 and teaches money as a measurement lesson in Grade 2. Ghana's money teaching is teacher evidence throughout — the JICA Basic 3 teacher manual works ways to pay and give change with play money, and the DL workbook totals pictured cedi notes to GH¢465. Sierra Leone's money teaching is teacher evidence from its Primary lesson plans — buying, selling and change with counters in Primary 1, money addition and subtraction to Le 50,000 in Primary 4 — and because those plans pre-date the Leone's redenomination, this lesson uses their method with re-based examples. South Africa builds rand and cents through its Grades 1–3 workbooks, from matching amounts and change from R10 to decimal prices like R12,50. Iran introduces the rial, its coins and banknotes and bank hundred-bundles in Grade 2, and asks what set amounts can buy in Grade 3. Egypt composes amounts from notes of 1 to 200 LE in Grade 2, asks can-Sara-buy-it comparison questions, and saves over days and weeks towards a target. Cambodia climbs the riel ladder from two 50s = one 100 in Grade 1 to counting and exchanging notes in Grades 2–3 and its first buying with change in Grade 4. Kazakhstan teaches tenge denominations, price, exchanging and paying with coins across its Grade 1–3 books. Nicaragua teaches córdoba banknotes, equivalence and córdoba–centavo conversion in Grade 2 and the national currency, with 1 córdoba = 100 centavos and decimal amounts, in Grade 3. Jordan teaches its coins and equal-value coins in Grade 1 and the dinar and paper money in Grade 2. Ethiopia counts santim coins in Grade 1, adds 100 santim = 1 birr and birr notes in Grade 2, and lists, compares, adds and subtracts money in Grade 3. Nepal teaches coins and notes from Re 1 to Rs 1000, the paisa join, Mohar and Suka, and rupees-and-paisa arithmetic with change from Rs 100. South Sudan shows SSP notes 1 to 100 and a shop price list in Primary 1, gives change in Primary 2, and makes change from larger amounts in Primary 4. Mauritania runs coins to notes to small purchases across Grades 1–2 and buying and selling in Grade 4. Greece teaches euro coins in a classroom supermarket in Grade 1, orders the euro banknotes in Grade 2, and returns to banknotes, coins and decimals as money in Grade 4. Iraq is not counted: its filing states money is never a topic there — dinars appear only inside word problems — so Iraq is an explicit negative, not a source.

Syria teaches money in Syrian lira from Grade 1 Semester 2 — coins, then notes, then banknotes to 1,000 — always by components (what makes what), totalling mixed groups, and picture-data problems about enough money, change and saving towards a price.

Saudi Arabia teaches money in riyals from Grade 1 Part Two — recognising the 1, 2, 5 and 10, counting a set from the biggest denomination, paying the same amount in different ways and judging enough-or-not-enough — and extends it to the 50 and the 100-riyal note in Grade 2, with the older 1445 Term-3 volumes witnessing and adding test review items.

Malaysia's money chapter is about managing money rather than counting it: a five-step financial management process built on SMART goals. (Its worked family financial plans are filed under business-mathematics, and its insurance and taxation chapters are lessons of their own.)

Rwanda teaches money from Rwandan franc coins and notes in Year 2, through currency, saving, budgeting and four-operation word problems to 5 000 Frw in Year 3, profit and loss in Primary 4, money transfer, borrowing and foreign-exchange conversion in Primary 5, bank saving and investment in Primary 6, and discount, commission, profit and loss and dividends in Senior 1 — with the Year 3 teacher's guide adding cashless payment (cards and mobile money) as a discussion point.

Sudan meets money inside Grade 1's addition work, adding toy prices in Sudanese pounds.

India carries money through its NCERT ladder — Math-Magic Class 1–5 — including coins of India; paying with tens and ones; paying with notes and coins; price per litre from a pump display.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик I, II, III, IV, IX (Grades 1, 2, 3, 4, 9): money to 10; money chains / totals; money word problems to 100.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Contemos... 1, 2, 3 y 4* (Grade 1), *Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más...* (Grade 2), *Contando con los recursos* (Grade 4), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5) and *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6). From its books: from Grade 1: coins — counting coins of each denomination in a purse (1, 5, 10, 12½, 25, 50 céntimos) in a table; Karibay's given: 4 coins of 1 céntimo; from Grade 4: banknote problems — greatest number of Bs. 100 notes inside Bs. 3.245.786; Bs. 10 notes plus 12 Bs. 1 coins totalling Bs. 398.052 → 39.804 notes; 370×100 + 59×10 + 1 = 37.591 (all posed); from Grade 6: ledger — Community currency accounting: the guaicaipuro exchange ledger (see negative-numbers).

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 1: coins and banknotes: 1, 2, 5 afghani coins; 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50 afghani notes (Lesson 35); afghani amounts as tens and ones: making amounts and comparing amounts.

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 1 (teacher's book): Provov veten: make 10 lekë in different ways with 1-lekë and 5-lekë coins (5 + 5 = 10; five 1-lekë coins + 5…; Money: recognise all coin types and calculate the amount to pay using different-value coins (5, 10, 20, 50,…. from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .4 area: simple interest (interesi i thjeshtë) and the unitary method (metoda njësi), marketing/finance links.

Palestine enters through the pupil's books of its Ministry of Education (Curriculum Centre, Ramallah), all in Arabic — Mathematics Grade 1 in two parts (both fourth edition, 2020), Grade 10 in two parts (Part One third edition, 2020; Part Two second edition, 2019 — the held parts are different editions and are cited as such), and the Grade 12 Technological-stream book (التكنولوجي, first edition, 2019 — the book's own name for its stream, never "Information Technology"). No ISBN is printed in any of the five books. From its books: from Grade 1 Part One: money-flavoured subtraction word problem in dinars (have 6 dinars…) inside the Unit 4 review; spend exactly 20 qirsh on priced toys (5, 9, 10, 11, 5 qirsh): choose a combination; from Grade 1 Part Two: money as place value: buy with 1-dinar and 10-dinar notes (10 + 6, 10 + 5, 10 + 10 dinars); word problems in qirsh and dinars: savings boxes doubled by grandfather at Eid; unit review: with 17 dinars choose two toys whose prices together do not exceed the money.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 1 syllabus requires identifying the local coins and the $5, $10 and $20 bills, giving equivalent value in coins and bills up to $5, and making change up to $5; Class 2 extends this to all local coins and notes up to $100 and change from a given sum; Class 3 maintains the currency requirements and adds buying and selling, converting foreign currency to local currency and back, and (in the attainment targets) budgeting and saving money; Class 4 maintains the same programme. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 3: match equal amounts of Turkish money: two 5 TL notes ↔ a 10 TL note; four 25 kr ↔ one 1 TL coin; four 50 kr ↔ two 1 TL coins; six 1 TL coins ↔ a 5 TL note + 1 TL coin (one 7 TL box is left unmatched); canteen price list (water 10 TL, milk 16 TL, ayran 10 TL, simit 15 TL): work out what each child bought from how they paid — two 50 kr + three 5 TL = 16 TL is the milk; one 10 TL + five 1 TL = 15 TL is the simit; 100 kuruş = 1 TL; make exact prices with cut-out play money (Appendix 1): scissors 13 TL 75 kr, pencil 9 TL, eraser 6 TL 75 kr, sharpener 10 TL 25 kr, ruler 15 TL 50 kr, notebook 9 TL 50 kr; then show the same price in a different mix of money; pay for a shopping list two different ways: tick how many of each note/coin (20, 10, 5 TL, 1 TL, kuruş coins) make each item's price — tomatoes 48 TL worked, then parsley 2 bunches at 8 TL, potatoes 2 kg, onions 2 kg, apples 1 kg at their per-kg prices; pay for clothes with the fewest notes and coins: socks 60 TL = one 50 TL + one 10 TL worked; then a 170 TL shirt, 180 TL skirt, 300 TL shirt, 375 TL trousers, 465 TL shoes; who can afford it? craft paper 5 TL and a balloon 3 TL against four children's pictured money; how much more does one child need for the paper; which two children have equal amounts; equal amounts in different money: one group builds an amount with play money (Appendix 2), the other group rebuilds exactly the same amount with different notes (100 + 20 + 10 = 130 TL rebuilt as two 50s + three 10s); money as a national symbol and careful spending: what happens to a family budget and the country's economy if money is spent carelessly (discussion/writing, no calculation); true/false on lira and kuruş: 4 lira 25 kuruş = 425 kuruş; 750 kuruş = 7 lira 50 kuruş; four 25 kuruş = 1 lira; the smallest coin is 50 kuruş — false; the largest note is 50 lira — false; the largest coin is 1 lira — false; two 10 lira = four 5 lira; three children's pictured money totals judged (270 TL / 220 TL claims).

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: the Tanzanian system itself — shillings and cents at 1 shilling = 100 cents, the short form sh X.XX, and the denominations pictured (cent coins 5–50; shilling coins 10–500; notes 500, 1,000, 2,000) — then all four operations on money in shillings/cents columns to sh 1,000,000, and word problems of change, profit and shopping lists, with the @ symbol meaning "each item".

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 2 teacher-evidence items from 2 of the guides (Book 2, Book 5; Grades 2).

**Book 2:** *Le Monde des Maths 2, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 2, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 9,555,550 bytes. Same collection and July-2020 revised programme as Book 1; items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where the sommaire prints them, later lessons cited by sequence (seq.). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 5:** *L'Explicite en Maths CE1, Guide pédagogique* (CE1 = Grade 2) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 18,580,797 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 2 programme corresponding to Book 2. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Money: the dirham and the centime (Monde des Maths 2 guide, lesson 28, seq. — the sommaire prints "La mémoire", read in the body as the money lesson) | Morocco — *Le Monde des Maths 2, Guide de l'enseignant(e), Grade 2* (teacher guide) — teacher evidence, cited by sequence (seq. — the sommaire prints no folio) |
| Money (Explicite CE1 guide) | Morocco — *L'Explicite en Maths CE1, Guide pédagogique, Grade 2* (teacher guide) — teacher evidence, printed p. 100 |

Morocco enters this lesson as teacher evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every book in Morocco's in-hand set is a teacher guide or teacher edition, not a pupil textbook, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as a pupil's book (the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent). Morocco's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 32 guides are read and stocked, but no pupil book is in hand.

Placements: liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-2026-11-10/maths/morocco-placements.md.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 6 items (4 from student books, 2 teacher evidence) from Book S2, Book S4, Book S5, Book T4, Book S7, Book T6.

**Book S2:** *Matemática, Libro de texto, 1.er grado, Tomo 2* (student textbook, Grade 1, Tomo 2, Unidades 6–11) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); held as two part-files (LT1_02_01: 11,881,880 bytes, 111 PDF pp.; LT1_02_02: 7,896,753 bytes, 110 PDF pp.). The tomo's printed pagination restarts; pages cited are its own printed folios.

**Book S4:** *Matemática, Libro de texto, 2.° grado, Tomo 2* (student textbook, Grade 2, Tomo 2, Unidades 5–9) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); held as two part-files (LT2_02_01: 11,920,331 bytes, 87 PDF pp.; LT2_02_02: 10,144,786 bytes, 68 PDF pp.). The tomo's printed pagination restarts; pages cited are its own printed folios.

**Book S5:** *Libro de texto, 3.° grado* (student textbook, Grade 3, MINED 2026-tree edition) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); 14,754,711 bytes, 196 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

**Book T4:** *Guía metodológica, 3.° grado* (teacher guide, Grade 3) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); held as two files (GM3_01: 12,698,931 bytes, 228 PDF pp.; GM3_02: 10,993,928 bytes, 222 PDF pp.). Cited by unit, and by the guide's own printed page where the read gives one. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide reproduces the student book's lessons with lesson-by-lesson teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test (*Prueba de la unidad*) per unit; it is cited as teacher evidence, never as a pupil textbook.

**Book S7:** *Libro de texto, 5.° grado* (student textbook, Grade 5, MINED 2026-tree edition) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); 18,263,367 bytes, 196 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

**Book T6:** *Guía metodológica, 5.° grado* (teacher guide, Grade 5) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); held as two files (GM5_01: 12,344,677 bytes, 244 PDF pp.; GM5_02: 12,471,242 bytes, 244 PDF pp.). Cited by unit. ## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 2 teacher-evidence items from Book 1.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Andorra's Primera ensenyança programme requires: (1r) the monetary system (euro–cent) | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 31 |
| Andorra's Primera ensenyança programme requires: (1r) simulating change in the monetary system | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 32 |

Andorra enters this lesson as teacher evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every book in Andorra's in-hand set is an official curriculum programme, not a pupil textbook, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as a pupil's book (the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent). Andorra's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 programmes are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand — none exists to hold for the Escola Andorrana — and the Spanish and French systems' maths manuals are list-only names with no files held.

## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 4 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Manual da 1.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 1) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Editora Moderna, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-267-0; 4,293,873 bytes, 81 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1).

**Book 2:** *Matemática — Manual da 2.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 2) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-297-8; 3,437,647 bytes, 56 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 23 calls the minuend “diminuendo ou aditivo” — “aditivo” is wrong for subtraction; the course teaches minuend. Editorial production notes are printed as lesson content on printed p. 50 — production matter, cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 3:** *Matemática — Manual da 3.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 3) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Progresso Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-270-0; 1,798,728 bytes, 85 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 21 repeats the minuend misnomer “diminuendo ou aditivo” (teach: minuend); printed p. 36's decimal-multiplication worked example is internally inconsistent — it states 4,15 × 2,3, lays out 3,15 and prints 7,245 (correct: 4,15 × 2,3 = 9,545); the exercise under “Noção de circunferência” at printed p. 44 repeats the parallel-lines exercise verbatim (a copy-paste error in the book); printed p. 62 prints the false line “20 m = 200 cm” (correct: 20 m = 2 000 cm); printed p. 64's formula under decâmetro repeats “1 hm = 100 m” (correct: 1 dam = 10 m).

**Book 4:** *Matemática — Manual da 4.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 4) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-296-1; 1,983,954 bytes, 112 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 43 claims division “is only possible if the dividend is a multiple of the divisor” (exact division only) — contradicted by Book 3's own division-with-remainder teaching; the course teaches division with remainder. Editorial production notes are printed as page content on printed pp. 17, 18, 21, 24, 25, 28, 30 and 45 — production matter, cited nowhere; the Tema 3 heading is misprinted inside the book (at printed p. 102); PDF pp. 106–112 are blank/back matter apart from the bibliografia (printed p. 107), cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 13 requirement-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2 and Book 3.

**Book 1:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 1–3* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,558,181 bytes, 263 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 51. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 3:** *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* (examination syllabus) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Youth & Sports, Testing & Evaluation Section; 2,351,047 bytes, 10 PDF pages (a scanned-image PDF with no text layer, read visually page by page); printed page = PDF page − 1. The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). SYLLABUS EVIDENCE ONLY — an examination syllabus states what candidates are required to know; it teaches nothing itself.

The Bahamas' class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 in-hand documents are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand; it advances only when a pupil book file — a Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* volume, or the Akhepran Grade 7 BJC textbook whose exact link already sits in the download list — lands in hand and is read.

## Australia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Australia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 books, all of them student textbooks** (Years 8–10: eight Cambridge volumes — the Essential Mathematics Australian Curriculum, Victorian Curriculum and CambridgeMATHS NSW lines, plus the GOLD support edition and the CORE pathway volume — and one Jacaranda volume, Maths Quest 10+10A) — and placed 844 teaching items. Australia has no government textbook series (ACARA writes the Australian Curriculum but publishes and prescribes no textbooks); the set is the school-used commercial series the country records name, and every book is cited as such. All 9 files are distinct works (distinct ISBNs, extents and contents, verified per book on the read); the publisher-line relations are stated in the placements: Book A1 (GOLD) is the support edition of Book A2's Year 8 material, Book A3 is the same line in its Victorian ©2024 edition, Books A4 and A6 are the NSW syllabus editions, and Book A9 is Book A8's CORE pathway companion. In the sections below the books are cited as Books A1–A9. Australia COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every item is pupil-textbook teaching. The read's four flagged claims are carried exactly: (F1) Book A3's contents lists its Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the file in hand contains the full chapter, bound after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941) — it is read and placed, and it founds this course's algorithmic-thinking lesson; (F2) Book A7's contents lists a Chapter 18, Algorithmic thinking, with no page numbers, and the chapter is NOT in the file (searched end to end; the book closes with its Glossary, printed p. 1106, and Index, printed p. 1117) — recorded as absent, not read, and no item rests on it; (F3) Book A9's running head at printed p. 644 prints section 10E's code as “0E” — the book's own production error; the placement cites 10E, as the section's heading and contents give it; (F4) only the 9 Maths books are this read's set — the country records' wider Australia picture (12 staged books across subjects, plus filled-by-exact-link titles) is not merged into this count, and in particular the two senior Maths titles that exist only as exact-link records (Cambridge Senior Specialist Mathematics, QCE Units 3&4, 2e, and Cambridge Senior Mathematical Methods, QCE Units 3&4, 2e) are NOT in hand and were NOT read. The placements' named exclusions — front matter and “how to use this book” guides; every chapter's pre-tests, progress quizzes, unlettered applications / problem-solving / modelling / investigation spreads, puzzles and games, chapter summaries, checklists and reviews, semester reviews, glossaries, indexes and answer sections — are apparatus/assessment, teach no new content, and are stocked nowhere. The set is Years 8–10 secondary only, so the primary lessons (counting, place value, first steps) receive no Australia item — that is the set's shape, not a reading gap. Australia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (9/9) has been read and placed; the two senior QCE exact-link titles do not block the in-hand class (if exact-link titles are ever ruled in-scope for the courses reckoning, Australia drops to PARTIAL until they are grabbed and read). Into this lesson the placements stock 3 items from Book A5, Book A6, Book A9.

**Book A5:** *Essential Mathematics for the Australian Curriculum Year 9, Third Edition* — Australia, Greenwood, Woolley, Goodman et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2020, ISBN 978-1-108-77288-4; 148,251,307 bytes, 848 PDF pp. Year 9 volume of the same Essential Mathematics AC line as Book A2. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 18).

**Book A6:** *CambridgeMATHS NSW Stage 5 (Year 9), Second Edition* — Australia, Palmer, Greenwood, Woolley et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2019, ISBN 978-1-108-46816-9; 114,064,205 bytes, 743 PDF pp. The NSW Stage 5 (pathways 5.1/5.2/5.3 in one volume) Year 9 book — the NSW counterpart of Book A5. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 18). Its Chapter 5 contents columns interleave in extraction; its section folios were each verified individually on the read.

**Book A9:** *Essential Mathematics for the Australian Curriculum CORE Year 10* — Australia, Greenwood et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2021, ISBN 978-1-108-87886-9; 32,097,354 bytes, 783 PDF pp. The CORE pathway Year 10 volume: the applied/foundation companion to Book A8 — a distinct work (different chapter set: consumer arithmetic, budgeting, Maths@Work career spreads), not a subset reprint of Book A8. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 14). Book error (flag F3): the running head at printed p. 644 prints section 10E's code as “0E”; the section is cited as 10E, as its own heading and the contents give it.

## Bhutan added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Bhutan's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **16 files: 13 student books and 3 teacher's guides of the Royal Government of Bhutan's *Understanding Mathematics* series (Department of School Education / Ministry of Education and Skills Development), English-medium throughout** — and placed 657 pupil teaching items and 30 teacher-evidence items. **Set correction, proved by the read: the expansion map's count of 10 student + 3 teacher is WRONG — the map counted only the textbooks.tsv snapshot (PP, I, II, IV–X) and missed the Class III, XI and XII student books recorded as secured and pushed in the staging books.json files and the push state; the true in-hand set is 13 student + 3 teacher = 16 files, every one grabbed and verified (byte size on every file; MD5 against books.json on the 11 files that carry one — all match; page counts match every record) and read in full.** The Class I staging-path collision is resolved: the main-branch grade-01 file is the student activity book (Book 3); the Class I teacher's guide (Book 4) is a separate, MD5-verified file that books.json records under the same staging path name. **Critical filing flag, carried exactly: both BHSEC filings claim "complete book, not a sample/preview" — FALSE. The Class XI file holds only chapters 1–5 of the 26 its own contents lists and the Class XII file only chapters 1–4 of the 19 its contents lists; only the chapters actually present were placed, and both books are cited as the partial excerpts they are.** The 3 teacher's guides are teacher evidence only: their items are named below as teacher evidence and are never counted as pupil teaching. The Class 2 activity book's Summative Assessment Recording Sheets (printed pp. 99–118) are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The pupil Chapter 9 pages open at Lesson 3 (Interpreting Bar Graphs) — no pupil Lessons 1–2 for that chapter appear in the file; the guide's parallel survey and data-collection lessons stand as teacher evidence instead. ## Costa Rica added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Costa Rica's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **25 books, all of them student books: the five CONED distance-secondary antologías *Antología de Matemáticas*, Niveles 7–11 (UNED/CONED — Nivel Sétimo 2017, Nivel Noveno 2018, Nivel 11 2019; Nivel Décimo by MSc. Jorge Díaz Porras) and the ITCR Proyecto EVEPRIM primary books for Grades 3 and 6 (10 textos + 10 folletos de prácticas across Relaciones y Álgebra, Números, Geometría, Medidas, Estadística and Probabilidad); 0 teacher guides for Costa Rica Maths exist in hand** — and placed 229 teaching items. **SET CORRECTION: the expansion map's count of 17 student is WRONG** — 17 is exactly the number of Costa Rica Mathematics rows in textbooks.tsv (10 EVEPRIM textos + only 2 of the 10 EVEPRIM folletos + 5 CONED antologías); the TSV was never updated for 8 folletos staged on 2026-10-25, and the staging books.json files and phase3-push-state.json agree on the true set of 25 (the folletos count student-side, per the map's own stated basis). All 25 were grabbed, verified (byte size + MD5 against books.json; the two Relaciones y Álgebra textos, which have no books.json entry, verified by size + title page as the main-repo files) and read in full — no partial excerpts, no duplicates (the SharePoint folder copies of the six texto books matched the staged sizes byte-for-byte, so they are the same files, cited once) and no unopenable files. The three Grade 3 textos were read in their November 2026 editions, the in-hand editions per books.json; the GitHub release mid-size-books-3 still holds the superseded October editions of those three textos (a record-level flag, reported, not fixed — same books, older exports; only the November editions are cited here). Two chapter promises in the CONED books are NOT teaching and place no item anywhere: CONED 7's Capítulo 4 is titled Estadística y Probabilidad but teaches statistics only (probability appears only in its introductory discussion questions, pp. 209–214), and CONED 10's Capítulo 1 objectives promise plane transformations that no Tema in the chapter teaches (only a recall of Grade 8 homothety). The placements' named exclusions — front matter, indices, bibliographies and answer keys (soluciones); the EVEPRIM answer-key chapters and the review chapters that re-teach already-placed sections; the CONED chapter motto/introduction pages and “desafío matemático” pages; and the videos and web interactives the EVEPRIM books link to — are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The 5 CONED book-error groups found in the read — CONED 7: the angle table (p. 106) labels a 360° angle “CONVEXO”, the inverse-proportionality example (pp. 201–203) states v·t = 247.5 for a product of 330 and “very close to 3 hours” at 78 km/h for ≈4.23 h with garbled fraction work, the triangle inequality (p. 125) omits “any two” sides, the median answer (p. 238) gives 68 for a median of 67, the worked powers page (p. 64) misaligns its results, and a tiling exercise (p. 35) reads “largo por largo”; CONED 8: the table of contents numbers the equations Tema “Tema 4” while the body heads it “Tema 5” (numbering slip only); CONED 9: the SI prefix table (p. 74) gives exa the symbol “R” (exa is E), the radian-to-degree rule (p. 117) is printed inverted/garbled though its worked examples are correct, and the contents duplicates “Función Cuadrática” for Capítulo 3 Temas 1 and 4 and mislabels Capítulo 4 as “Capítulo III”; CONED 10: the falling-body model (p. 373) prints “12” for ½ (the worked values use ½ correctly), the key (p. 42) duplicates “tangentes” where “secantes” is meant, p. 14 asks for the area of a “circunferencia” (the circle), and the pentagon solution (p. 86) drops its degree symbols; CONED 11: the logarithm table (p. 118) misaligns its examples (it prints log₅ 5 = 2, which belongs to log₅ 25), the wound-model solution (p. 172) shows an unsigned division display before the correct result, and “invectiva” (p. 121) is a typo for inyectiva — are carried corrected on the rows they touch. One further note is carried as the placements carry it: the Medidas G3 answer key's building-height estimate reads 45 dam in the October copy and “4 dam” in the November extraction — treated as a re-export/extraction artefact in the key, not teaching content, and cited nowhere. Costa Rica's books are pupil books, so Costa Rica is COUNTED: it joins this lesson's acceptance count wherever the placements stock an item. CONED printed page = PDF page throughout; EVEPRIM pages are cited by PDF page with the book's section numbers (two exceptions print folios: the EVEPRIM 6 Probabilidad texto's printed folio = PDF page − 6 and the EVEPRIM 3 Medidas texto's = PDF page − 7; citations give PDF pages). Into this lesson the placements stock 2 items (items 193 and 226).

**Book 1:** *Medidas, 3º — Folleto de prácticas* — Costa Rica, Escuela de Matemática, ITCR, Proyecto EVEPRIM 3.2; 21,471,558 bytes, 45 PDF pp.; a compilation of the practices of the Medidas texto. Pages cited by PDF page. Pupil practice book.

**Book 2:** *Medidas, 3º* (texto) — Costa Rica, Escuela de Matemática, ITCR, Proyecto EVEPRIM 3.2; November 2026 high-resolution edition (the in-hand edition per the record, replacing the October copy of 31,668,485 bytes / 213 pp — same book, same five teaching chapters, repaginated; the October copy was also read and agrees in content); 319,391,831 bytes, 205 PDF pp. Printed folio = PDF page − 7; citations give PDF pages. Pupil textbook.

## Ireland added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Ireland's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 pupil textbooks of the Text & Tests series (The Celtic Press / CJ Fallon): one English volume, Text & Tests 4 (New Edition), and eight Irish-language (Téacs & Trialacha) volumes** — and placed 570 items. Ireland has no government-published textbooks: every book in the set is a commercial in-use title held as a complete free copy. The English TT4 was read in phase3-staging (byte- and SHA-256-verified in place); the eight Irish volumes were re-grabbed from their recorded COGG (cogg.ie) source URLs, each landing byte-exact at its recorded size, every file's identity proved from its cover, copyright and contents pages. The expansion map's count is right: **9 student + 0 teacher** — the one further Ireland file in hand, *Dúchas 2 — Leabhar Scileanna*, is a Junior Cycle Irish-language (Gaeilge) skills book, not Maths, and is cited nowhere in this course. Extent, honestly: five whole-course books (TT1, TT2 GL, TT3, English TT4, TT6); three strand volumes complete as published but covering one strand only (TT4 Irish = Strand 2; TT5 Irish = Strand 1; TT7 Irish = Strand 5); and one supplement booklet (the TT5 Forlíonadh — deferred Strand 1 material and the Central Limit Theorem), complete as published, not a standalone textbook. Two record flags ride with the set (the read's flagged claims F1–F2, both about the library records, not the course): **F1** — 8 of the 9 PDFs are absent from their phase3-staging folders, which hold only books.json + CURRICULUM.md; all eight are secured in GitHub and were re-grabbed byte-exact from COGG for the read, and restoring them into staging is owed on the library side. **F2** — the held TT3 is the updated edition (2024) of the 2013 book, with an added Chapter 19, Inferential Statistics, that no record mentions; its three items are stocked from that chapter and TT3's book entry names the edition wherever it appears. The older strand books and the English TT4 New Edition teach much of the same Leaving Certificate Higher Level ground in two languages and editions (e.g. TT6 chs. 1–2 ≈ TT4 Eng chs. 1–2; TT7 ch. 2 ≈ TT4 Eng ch. 7): each book's row is stocked as its own citation — they are distinct held books, not duplicates — while Ireland itself is counted once in this lesson's taught-in figure. Counting grain: one item per distinct teaching section of each book. The placements' named exclusions — every book's chapter tests (“Cuir triail ort féin”), tasks, learning-experience and review sections, chapter summaries and Answers sections, and every book's prefaces, keyword lists and contents pages — are stocked nowhere. Ireland COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every Ireland book is a pupil textbook. ## Fiji added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Fiji's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **13 pupil textbooks of the Ministry of Education / Curriculum Development Unit series, one for each of Years 1–13** — and placed 1,012 items. The expansion map's count is exact: staging `books.json` holds exactly 13 Mathematics entries (one per year, all `kind: student`) and `phase3-push-state.json` lists all 13 as pushed; no Maths teacher guide is in hand (the AQEP Maths Guides for Lower Primary exist only as AnyFlip reader copies in the download list — not in hand, not counted). The staged PDFs had been pushed and deleted locally, so each book was re-fetched for the read — from the repo tree (Contents API) where the pusher had stored it as a blob, else from the book's recorded `source_url` (a Wayback Machine capture of the ministry file, Beacon Media, or Google Drive) — and the grabbed copies were deleted after their placements were written. The read's nine flagged claims ride with the set, honestly: (1) `textbooks.tsv` carries only 9 of the 13 rows (Years 1, 2, 3, 4, 7, 10, 11, 12, 13) — Years 5, 6, 8 and 9 are staged and pushed but missing from the TSV; (2) the Year 2 scan duplicates printed p. 57 and printed p. 58 is absent, so the Money unit's Activity 12 is not in hand to read and is cited nowhere; (3) the Year 5 capture has no cover/title leaves — the file opens at the Strand 1 divider and the body runs complete, Strand 1 p. 1 to Strand 5 p. 98, its identity resting on the staged record and the body's own structure; (4) Year 4's folio sequence is irregular — folios 44–48 and 54 never appear and folios 42 and 55 each appear twice — but the leaf count closes exactly (85 printed numbers − 6 absent + 2 duplicated = 81 body leaves), every contents strand and unit is present and no activity breaks mid-stream at the gaps, so the evidence points to the printed book's own folio misprints rather than missing leaves, a judgement the file alone cannot prove; (5) Year 3's scan order is scrambled at the end (contents folio ii and printed pp. 46, 51, 52 sit at PDF pp. 76–80) and one activity page appears twice (folioed 47 and 51 — counted once), with folio tracking accounting for all of printed pp. 1–75; (6) Year 10 exists in two packagings — the staged Beacon Media file (9,099,595 bytes, 202 pp.) is the copy cited; an ECopies capture of the same book is 9,079,205 bytes / 200 pp.; (7) Year 12's first Wayback transfer arrived truncated (11,794,148 bytes, broken cross-reference table) and was discarded — a fresh download delivered the complete book (17,085,310 bytes, 284 pp.), which is what was read; (8) Year 1's contents lists Chance at p. 65 and Data at p. 66 where the folios are 64 and 65 — the body count closes exactly, so nothing is missing and the contents is one out; (9) Year 6's interior copyright and acknowledgement pages misname it the “Year 7” book — a copy-paste error in the book itself; its cover metadata, contents, running heads and body are Year 6 throughout, and it is read and placed as Year 6. Book errors found in the read are taught corrected, the corrected form being what the items teach — among them Year 3's conversion-table lines “1,000 cm = 1 km” (correct: 100,000 cm) and “10,000 mm = 1,000 m” (correct: 10 m), Year 4's banner “30 minutes = 1 hour” against its own body's 60, Year 5's “1 000 millimetres = 1 litre”, Year 9's contents “Common Multitudes” (for Common Multiples) and its (a × b)ᵐ = aᵐ × bⁿ misprint, and Year 1's count-in-twos strip printing 9 where 19 belongs; Year 10 carries its own printed errata list (pp. 201–202), whose corrections are noted in the items. Counting grain: one item per distinct teaching point — a named concept, method, rule or worked skill with its printed page. The placements' named exclusions — strand divider pages; contents and acknowledgement pages; pure exercise sets and worked-example repeats of an item already placed; Year 1's “Checking my number” ten-frame reference page (printed p. 32); Year 3's duplicated capacity page (counted once); Year 4's closing multiplication-table chart (printed p. 86); Year 10's printed errata list (pp. 201–202, apparatus — its corrections ride in the items); Year 12's closing motivational quotes and exam tips (printed pp. 273–275); Year 13's bibliography and back matter — are stocked nowhere. Fiji COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every Fiji book is a pupil textbook.
